<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->

<div class='question question'>

Work out $4802 \div 7$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 4802 \div 7 \\\\
&= \dfrac{4802}{7} \\\\
&= \dfrac{686 \times 7}{7} \\\\
&= \dfrac{686 \times \cancel 7}{\cancel 7} \\\\
&= 686
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$686$

</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

&= \dfrac{686 \times 7}{7} \\\\
&= \dfrac{686 \times \cancel 7}{\cancel 7} \\\\
&= 686
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Work out $\dfrac{3}{4}$ of $96$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3} {4} \ \text {of} \ 96 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3} {4} \times 96 \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 96} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 24 \times 4} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 24 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
&= 3 \times 24 \\\\
&= 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

&= \dfrac {3 \times 24 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\

&= 72
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Write down the next number in the sequence.

$83, \quad 76, \quad 69, \quad 62, \quad \boxed{\phantom{11}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by subtracting $7$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-7)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-7)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-7)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-7)}}  \\
83,  &&   76,  &&   69,  &&   62,  &&   \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Missing number}       &= 62 - 7 \\\\
                            &= 55
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$55$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\begin {aligned}
&= 14050 - 300 \\\\
&= 13750
\end {aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

In Moscow, the temperature is $-11 \degree \text{C}$ and in Paris the temperature is $14 \ \text{degrees}$ warmer.


What is the temperature in Paris?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Temperature in Paris}  &= \text {Temperature in Moscow} + 14 \\\\
                              &= -11 + 14 \\\\
                              &= 3 \degree \text {C}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \degree \text {C}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Temperature in Paris}  &= \text {Temperature in Moscow} + 14 \\\\
                              &= -11 + 14 \\\\
                              &= 3 \degree \text {C}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Aisha thinks of a number.

She adds four and then multiplies by $8$.

Her result is $56$.


What was the number Aisha first thought of? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number Aisha thought of $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
(a + 4) \times 8  &= 56 \\\\
a + 4             &= \dfrac {56} {8} \\\\
a + 4             &= \dfrac {8 \times 7} {8} \\\\
a + 4             &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 7} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a + 4             &= 7 \\\\
a                 &= 7 - 4 \\\\
a                 &= 3
\end {aligned}
$

Number Alisha thought of $= 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number Aisha thought of $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
(a + 4) \times 8  &= 56 \\\\
a + 4             &= \dfrac {56} {8} \\\\
a + 4             &= \dfrac {8 \times 7} {8} \\\\
a + 4             &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 7} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a + 4             &= 7 \\\\
a                 &= 7 - 4 \\\\
a                 &= 3
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number Alisha thought of $= 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Georgie left home at $7{:}55 \ \text{am}$ and reached school $45 \ \text{minutes}$ later.

At what time did Georgie reach school?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}  
\text {Arrival time}               &= 7 {:} 55 \ \text {am} + 45 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                   &= 8 {:} 40 \ \text {am}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 {:} 40 \ \text {am}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}  
\text {Arrival time}               &= 7 {:} 55 \ \text {am} + 45 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                   &= 8 {:} 40 \ \text {am}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>

<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1:1:14</p>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

What is the difference between a tenth of $5$ and a fifth of $10$? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Difference}  &=  \dfrac {1} {5} \ \text {of} \ 10 - \dfrac {1} {10} \ \text {of} \ 5 \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times 10 - \dfrac {1} {10} \times 5 \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10} {5} - \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10 \times 2} {5 \times 2} - \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {20} {10} - \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {20 - 5} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {15} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {5 \times 3} {5 \times 2} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{5} \times 3} {\cancel{5} \times 2} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3} {2}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {3} {2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Difference}  &=  \dfrac {1} {5} \ \text {of} \ 10 - \dfrac {1} {10} \ \text {of} \ 5 \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times 10 - \dfrac {1} {10} \times 5 \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10} {5} - \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10 \times 2} {5 \times 2} - \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {20} {10} - \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {20 - 5} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {15} {10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {5 \times 3} {5 \times 2} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{5} \times 3} {\cancel{5} \times 2} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3} {2}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {3} {2}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

&= (13 + 7) \times 4 \\\\

<div class='workings'>

Let the input $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 7) \times 4        &= 120 \\\\
a + 7                   &= \dfrac{120}{4} \\\\
a + 7                   &= \dfrac{30 \times 4}{4} \\\\
a + 7                   &= \dfrac{30 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
a + 7                   &= 30 \\\\
a                       &= 30 - 7 \\\\
a                       &= 23
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

There are two possible function machines that will give the results shown below.

Work out the rules for the two possible machines.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-15-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-15-subquestion-2-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-15-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

**Rule 1**

Let first label $= + a$

Let second label $= \times b$

$
\begin{aligned}
(5 + a) \times b &= 4 \\\\
5b + ab &= 4 \\\\
(8 + a) \times b &= 10 \\\\
8b + ab &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
8b + ab &= 10 \\\\
8b + (4 - 5b) &= 10 \\\\
8b - 5b &= 10 - 4 \\\\
3b &= 6 \\\\
b &= \dfrac{6}{3} \\\\
b &= \dfrac{2 \times 3}{3} \\\\
b &= \dfrac{2 \times \cancel 3}{\cancel 3} \\\\
b &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
5b + ab &= 4 \\\\
5 \times 2 + 2 \times a &= 4 \\\\
10 + 2a &= 4 \\\\
2a &= 4 - 10 \\\\
2a &= -6 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{-6}{2} \\\\
a &= -3
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore,

The rule $= - 3 \ \text{then}\ \times 2$

**Rule 2**

Let first label $= \times a$

Let second label $= + b$

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times a + b &= 4 \\\\
5a + b &= 4 \\\\
8 \times a + b &= 10 \\\\
8a + b &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
8a + b &= 10 \\\\
8a + (4 - 5a) &= 10 \\\\
8a - 5a &= 10 - 4 \\\\
3a &= 6 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{6}{3} \\\\
a &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
5a + b &= 4 \\\\
5 \times 2 + b &= 4 \\\\
10 + b &= 4 \\\\
b &= 4 - 10 \\\\
b &= -6
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore,

The rule $= \times 2 \ \text{then}\ - 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-3 \ \text{then}\ \times 2 \\\\ \text{Or,}\ \times 2 \ \text{then}\ - 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>**Rule 1**
<emptyline>
Let first label $= + a$
<emptyline>
Let second label $= \times b$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(5 + a) \times b &= 4 \\\\
5b + ab &= 4 \\\\
(8 + a) \times b &= 10 \\\\
8b + ab &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

8b + (4 - 5b) &= 10 \\\\
8b - 5b &= 10 - 4 \\\\
3b &= 6 \\\\
b &= \dfrac{6}{3} \\\\
b &= \dfrac{2 \times 3}{3} \\\\
b &= \dfrac{2 \times \cancel 3}{\cancel 3} \\\\
b &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve for \'a\'
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5b + ab &= 4 \\\\
5 \times 2 + 2 \times a &= 4 \\\\
10 + 2a &= 4 \\\\
2a &= 4 - 10 \\\\
2a &= -6 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{-6}{2} \\\\
a &= -3
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore,
<emptyline>
The rule $= - 3 \ \text{then}\ \times 2$
<emptyline>
**Rule 2**
<emptyline>
Let first label $= \times a$
<emptyline>
Let second label $= + b$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times a + b &= 4 \\\\
5a + b &= 4 \\\\
8 \times a + b &= 10 \\\\
8a + b &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve for \'a\'
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
8a + b &= 10 \\\\
8a + (4 - 5a) &= 10 \\\\
8a - 5a &= 10 - 4 \\\\
3a &= 6 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{6}{3} \\\\
a &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve for \'b\'
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5a + b &= 4 \\\\
5 \times 2 + b &= 4 \\\\
10 + b &= 4 \\\\
b &= 4 - 10 \\\\
b &= -6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore,
<emptyline>
The rule $= \times 2 \ \text{then}\ - 6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

| $51348$ 	| $51348 - 50000 = 1348$ 	|

<div class='question question'>

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-17-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-17-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-17-00.png")

<div class='question question'>

Lily uses these ingredients to make strawberry sorbet:

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-19-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-19-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-19-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate the ingredients needed to make this sorbet for $15$ people.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{people}     &:& \text{water}                                         &:& \text{sugar}                                                                          &:& \text{strawberries}                                  &:& \text{eggs} \\\\
= & 6                 &:& 300 \ \text{ml}                                      &:& 100 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 450 \ \text{g}                                       &:& 2 \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {300} {6}                                     &:& \dfrac {100} {6}                                                                      &:& \dfrac {450} {6}                                     &:& \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 15       &:& \dfrac {300 \times 15} {6}                           &:& \dfrac {100 \times 15} {6}                                                            &:& \dfrac {450 \times 15} {6}                           &:& \dfrac {2 \times 15} {6} \\\\
= & 15                &:& \dfrac {6 \times 50 \times 15} {6}                   &:& \dfrac {50 \times 2 \times 5 \times 3} {3 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {6 \times 75 \times 15} {6}                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 5} {3 \times 2} \\\\
= & 15                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 50 \times 15} {\cancel 6}   &:& \dfrac {50 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 75 \times 15} {\cancel 6}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times 5} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 15                &:& 50 \times 15                                         &:& 50 \times 5                                                                           &:& 75 \times 15                                         &:& 5 \\\\
= & 15                &:& 750 \ \text{ml}                                      &:& 250 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 1125 \ \text{g}                                      &:& 5
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Water $=$ $750 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Sugar $=$ $250 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Strawberries $=$ $1125 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Eggs $=$ $5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{people}     &:& \text{water}                                         &:& \text{sugar}                                                                          &:& \text{strawberries}                                  &:& \text{eggs} \\\\
= & 6                 &:& 300 \ \text{ml}                                      &:& 100 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 450 \ \text{g}                                       &:& 2 \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {300} {6}                                     &:& \dfrac {100} {6}                                                                      &:& \dfrac {450} {6}                                     &:& \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 15       &:& \dfrac {300 \times 15} {6}                           &:& \dfrac {100 \times 15} {6}                                                            &:& \dfrac {450 \times 15} {6}                           &:& \dfrac {2 \times 15} {6} \\\\
= & 15                &:& \dfrac {6 \times 50 \times 15} {6}                   &:& \dfrac {50 \times 2 \times 5 \times 3} {3 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {6 \times 75 \times 15} {6}                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 5} {3 \times 2} \\\\
= & 15                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 50 \times 15} {\cancel 6}   &:& \dfrac {50 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 75 \times 15} {\cancel 6}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times 5} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 15                &:& 50 \times 15                                         &:& 50 \times 5                                                                           &:& 75 \times 15                                         &:& 5 \\\\
= & 15                &:& 750 \ \text{ml}                                      &:& 250 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 1125 \ \text{g}                                      &:& 5
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

For how many people is Lily making this sorbet if she uses exactly $1 \ \text{kilogram}$ of sugar?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of people Lily served sorbet $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
100 \ \text{g} : 6 \ \text{people}              &= 1 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{people} \\\\
\dfrac{100 \ \text{g}} {6}                      &= \dfrac {1 \ \text{kg}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{100 \ \text{g}} {6}                      &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
100 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 6} {100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times 6} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

Number of people $= 60$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{sugar}                               &:& \text{people} \\\\
= & 100 \ \text{g}                             &:& 6 \\\\
= & 100 \times 10                              &:& 6 \times 10 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{g}                            &:& 60 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                              &:& 60
\end{array}
$

Number of people $= 60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of people Lily served sorbet $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
100 \ \text{g} : 6 \ \text{people}              &= 1 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{people} \\\\
\dfrac{100 \ \text{g}} {6}                      &= \dfrac {1 \ \text{kg}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{100 \ \text{g}} {6}                      &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
100 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 6} {100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times 6} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of people $= 60$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{sugar}                               &:& \text{people} \\\\
= & 100 \ \text{g}                             &:& 6 \\\\
= & 100 \times 10                              &:& 6 \times 10 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{g}                            &:& 60 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                              &:& 60
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of people $= 60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

Celia and Autumn have each thought of an integer (whole number) between $0$ and $20$.

The product of their numbers is $72$ and the difference between the numbers is the same as Celia’s number.

What is Autumn’s number? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Celia’s number $= a$

Let Autumn's number $= b$

As per the question, 

$ a \times b   = 72$

$b - a = a$ 

$b = 2a$

Using this in first equation, 

$
\begin {aligned}
a \times b   &= 72 \\\\
a \times 2a  &= 72 \\\\
2a ^ 2       &= 72 \\\\
a ^ 2        &= \dfrac {72} {2} \\\\
a ^ 2        &= \dfrac {36 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a ^ 2        &= \dfrac {36 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a ^ 2        &= 36 \\\\
a            &= \sqrt {36} \\\\
a            &= 6
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Autumn's number} &= 2 \times 6 \\\\ 
                       &= 12 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let Celia’s number $= a$

Let Autumn's number $= b$

As per the question, 

$ a \times b   = 72$

$b - a = a$ 

$b = 2a$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $72$.
- The one number is twice of second number.

Factors of $72 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 9, 12, 18, 24, 36, 72$

Factor pairs of $72 = (1, 72), (2, 36), (3,24), (4,18), (6, 12), (9, 8)$

| Factors   |            $b = 2a$               |      Product      |
|:-------:  |:--------------------------------: |:---------------:  |
| $1, 72$   |             No                    |       72          |
| $2, 36$   |             No                    |       72          |
| $3, 24$   |             No                    |       72          |
| $4, 18$   |             No                    |       72          |
| $6, 12$   |            Yes                    |       72          |
| $8,  9$   |             No                    |       72          |


A quick check shows that $6$ and $12$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

Number Autumn thought of $= 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Kiwi fruits cost $78 \text{p}$ each; cherries cost $8 \text{p}$ each.

Gina buys as many kiwi fruits as she can for $\pounds 5$, and spends all of her change on cherries. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many kiwi fruits does Gina buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


Lets convert all the costs into pence.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of fruits Gina bought }    &= \dfrac{\pounds 5}{78 \text{p}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac{5 \times 100 \text{p}}{78 \text{p}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac{5 \times 50 \times 2}{39 \times 2 \text{p}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac{5 \times 50 \times \cancel 2 \text{p}}{39 \times  \cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac{250}{39} \\\\
                                        &= 6 \dfrac{16}{39} \\\\
                                        &= 6
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
Lets convert all the costs into pence.
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of fruits Gina bought }    &= \dfrac{\pounds 5}{78 \text{p}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac{5 \times 100 \text{p}}{78 \text{p}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac{5 \times 50 \times 2}{39 \times 2 \text{p}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac{5 \times 50 \times \cancel 2 \text{p}}{39 \times  \cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac{250}{39} \\\\
                                        &= 6 \dfrac{16}{39} \\\\
                                        &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many cherries does Gina buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


Lets convert all the costs into pence.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Changes left } &= \pounds 5 - 78 \times 6 \text{p} \\\\
                     &= 500 \text{p} - 78 \times 6 \text{p} \\\\
                     &= 500 - 468 \\\\
                     &= 32
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cherries Gina bought}  &= \dfrac{32}{8} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{8 \times 4}{8} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{\cancel 8 \times 4}{\cancel 8} \\\\
                                       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
Lets convert all the costs into pence.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Changes left } &= \pounds 5 - 78 \times 6 \text{p} \\\\
                     &= 500 \text{p} - 78 \times 6 \text{p} \\\\
                     &= 500 - 468 \\\\
                     &= 32
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cherries Gina bought}  &= \dfrac{32}{8} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{8 \times 4}{8} \\\\
                                       &= \dfrac{\cancel 8 \times 4}{\cancel 8} \\\\
                                       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

James missed the $4.20 \ \text{p.m.}$ train by three minutes.

|             |       Train A      |       Train B      |
|:-----------:|:------------------:|:------------------:|
|    London   | $4.20 \ \text{p.m.}$ | $5.02 \ \text{p.m.}$ |
| Staplehurst | $5.16 \ \text{p.m.}$ | $5.59 \ \text{p.m.}$ |

How long must he wait to catch the 5.02 p.m. train?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time when James reached the station}      &= 4{:}20 + 3 \ \text{mins} \\\\
                                                &= 4{:}23 \\\\
\text{Waiting time}                             &= 5{:}02 - 4{:}23 \\\\
                                                &= 39 \ \text{mins}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$39 \ \text{mins}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time when James reached the station}      &= 4{:}20 + 3 \ \text{mins} \\\\
                                                &= 4{:}23 \\\\
\text{Waiting time}                             &= 5{:}02 - 4{:}23 \\\\
                                                &= 39 \ \text{mins}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$39 \ \text{mins}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A square has perimeter $20 \ \text{cm}$.

Work out the area of the square.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


Let the length of a side of square $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
4 \times a                  &= 20 \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                           &= 5 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Area the square}     &= a \times a \\\\
                            &= 5 \times 5 \\\\
                            &= 25 \ \text {cm} ^ 2
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25 \ \text {cm} ^ 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_square
<emptyline>
Let the length of a side of square $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
4 \times a                  &= 20 \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                           &= 5 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Area the square}     &= a \times a \\\\
                            &= 5 \times 5 \\\\
                            &= 25 \ \text {cm} ^ 2
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25 \ \text {cm} ^ 2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

What is the range of her marks?

<div class='workings'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Range}   &= 9 - 6 \\\\
                &= 3
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

                &= 3
\end {aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

The pictogram below shows the results of a survey into the numbers of woodlice found under pots in a greenhouse.

One symbol represents **two** woodlice.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many woodlice were found under pot $A$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Woodlice  under pot A}                    &= 7.5 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Woodlice  under pot A}                    &= 7.5 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What was the total number of woodlice found?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of woodlice}                       &= (7.5 + 4.5 + 5 + 3.5 + 4.5) \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 25 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 50
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of woodlice}                       &= (7.5 + 4.5 + 5 + 3.5 + 4.5) \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 25 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 50
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$50$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Charlotte removes a third of the woodlice from pot $B$ and $20\%$ of the woodlice from pot $C$. 
She puts these woodlice under a new pot, $F$.

How many woodlice are under pot $F$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Woodlice under pot F}             &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 4.5 \times 2 + 20 \% \times 5 \times 2  \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 9 + \dfrac {20} {100} \times 10  \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {9} {3} + \dfrac {20 \times 10} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 3} {3} + \dfrac {20 \times 10} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} + \dfrac {2 \cancel 0 \times 1 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                        &= 3 + 2 \\\\
                                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Woodlice under pot F}             &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 4.5 \times 2 + 20 \% \times 5 \times 2  \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 9 + \dfrac {20} {100} \times 10  \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {9} {3} + \dfrac {20 \times 10} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 3} {3} + \dfrac {20 \times 10} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} + \dfrac {2 \cancel 0 \times 1 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                        &= 3 + 2 \\\\
                                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

Mum, Dad and Granny have three cakes:

Chocolate, strawberry and coffee.

Mum likes chocolate or coffee.

Dad likes chocolate, and Granny likes all three.

How can it be arranged, so that they each get a cake that they like? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Since Dad likes only chocolate, he can have the the chocolate cake.

Mum can have coffee.

That leaves strawberry cake for Granny.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Dad $=$ Chocolate

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Mum $=$ Coffee

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Granny $=$ Strawberry

</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Zoya has a jug of capacity $1.5 \ \text{litres}$ which is full of lemonade.

She has eight cups each of capacity $150 \ \text{ml}$.

Zoya fills the cups with lemonade from her jug.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What volume of lemonade is left in the jug?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1.5 \ \text{liter}\ - 150 \ \text{ml}\ \times 8 \\\\
&= (1.5  \times 1000) \ \text{ml}\ - 150 \ \text{ml}\ \times 8 \\\\
&= 1500 - 1200 \\\\
&= 300 \ \text{ml}\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

300 ml

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1.5 \ \text{liter}\ - 150 \ \text{ml}\ \times 8 \\\\
&= (1.5  \times 1000) \ \text{ml}\ - 150 \ \text{ml}\ \times 8 \\\\
&= 1500 - 1200 \\\\
&= 300 \ \text{ml}\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Zoya has five $2 \ \text{litre}$ bottles of lemonade

How many times could Zoya completely fill her jug from these bottles?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total volume Zoya has} &= 5 \times 2 \text{litre} \\\\
&= 10 \text{litre}
\end{aligned}
$

Let number of times jug can be filled $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 1.5 &= 10 \\\\
a \times \dfrac{15}{10} &= 10 \\\\
a \times \dfrac{3}{2} &= 10 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{10}{\dfrac{3}{2}} \\\\
a &= 10 \times \dfrac{2}{3} \\\\
a &= \dfrac{20}{3} \\\\
a &= 6.66
\end{aligned}
$

Since the jug had to be completely filled,

Total times $= 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total volume Zoya has} &= 5 \times 2 \text{litre} \\\\

$

a &= \dfrac{20}{3} \\\\
a &= 6.66
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Since the jug had to be completely filled,
<emptyline>
Total times $= 6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the perimeter of the card?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of the card}  &= 2 \times (18 + 6) \\\\
                               &= 2 \times 24 \\\\
                               &= 48 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of the card}  &= 2 \times (18 + 6) \\\\
                               &= 2 \times 24 \\\\
                               &= 48 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the area of the card?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Area of the card}  &= 18 \times 6 \\\\
                          &= 108 \ \text {cm} ^ 2
\end {aligned} 
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$108 \ \text {cm} ^ 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Rectangular stickers measure $3 \ \text{cm}$ by $2 \ \text{cm}$.

Susan wants to cover the front of the card with stickers, without any overlapping.

What is the maximum number of stickers she can fit on the front of the card?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Maximum number of stickers that can fit on the card}  &= \dfrac {\text {Area of the card}} {\text {Area of a sticker}} \\\\
                                                             &= \dfrac {108} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                                                             &= \dfrac {108} {6} \\\\
                                                             &= \dfrac {18 \times 6} {6} \\\\
                                                             &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel {6}} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
                                                             &= 18 \ \text {stickers}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18 \ \text {stickers}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Maximum number of stickers that can fit on the card}  &= \dfrac {\text {Area of the card}} {\text {Area of a sticker}} \\\\
                                                             &= \dfrac {108} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                                                             &= \dfrac {108} {6} \\\\
                                                             &= \dfrac {18 \times 6} {6} \\\\
                                                             &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel {6}} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
                                                             &= 18 \ \text {stickers}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18 \ \text {stickers}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>
<div class='review-comments'>

<h4>Review Comments</h4>
PArt A 

Perimeter P capital = 2 x (length + width)

in step 3 take 24 out of brackets.

= 48 cm

do in blue answer too..



Part B 

not perimeter! Area of the card = lengthn x width

= 18 x 6

= 108 cm^2

do in blue answer too..



PArt C 

Maximum Stickers she can fit &= Area of card / Area of sticker

=108 / 3 x 2 

= 3 x 2 x 18 / 3 x 2 

= cancel now

 = 18 

Blue answer = 18 Stickers
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Cube $A$ has a volume of $8 \ \text{cm}^3$.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-38-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-38-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-38-00.png") 


Another cube, $B$, has edges which are twice the length of the edges of cube $A$.

Work out the volume of cube $B$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Volume of a cube} = (\text{Side}) ^ { 3 }$


Let the side of cube A $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a^3                     &= 8 \\\\
\text{Volume of cube B} &= (2a)^3 \\\\
                        &= 8a^3 \\\\
                        &= 8 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= 64 \ \text{cm}^3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$64 \ \text{cm}^3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_volume_cube
<emptyline>
Let the side of cube A $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a^3                     &= 8 \\\\
\text{Volume of cube B} &= (2a)^3 \\\\
                        &= 8a^3 \\\\
                        &= 8 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= 64 \ \text{cm}^3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$64 \ \text{cm}^3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What would be the mass of $1000$ tea leaves?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mass of 1000 tea leaves}  &= \text {Mass of one tea leaf} \times 1000 \\\\
                                 &= 0.008 \times 1000 \\\\
                                 &= 8 \ \text {grams}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text {grams}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mass of 1000 tea leaves}  &= \text {Mass of one tea leaf} \times 1000 \\\\
                                 &= 0.008 \times 1000 \\\\
                                 &= 8 \ \text {grams}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \ \text {grams}$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many tea leaves would you expect there to be in a $1 \ \text{kg}$ pack of tea?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea leaves}      &= \dfrac {1000} {0.008} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 1000} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                  &= 1000 \times 125 \\\\
                                  &= 125000
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$125000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea leaves}      &= \dfrac {1000} {0.008} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 1000} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                  &= 1000 \times 125 \\\\
                                  &= 125000
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$125000$
</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A tea bag contains $250$ tea leaves.

The tissue covering weighs $0.5 \ \text{g}$.

How many tea bags will there be in a $250 \text{g}$ packet of tea bags?

(The $250 \text{g}$ includes the mass of the bags and not just the tea leaves.)

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea bags}        &= \dfrac {\text {Total mass}} {\text{Mass of 1 tea bag}} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {250 \times 0.008 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {25 \times 10 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 10 \times 10} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                                  &= 100
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$100 \ \text {bags}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea bags}        &= \dfrac {\text {Total mass}} {\text{Mass of 1 tea bag}} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {250 \times 0.008 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {25 \times 10 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 10 \times 10} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                                  &= 100
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$100 \ \text {bags}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1:1:42</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

talgebra

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Three hedgehogs, Rana, Sarah and Tina, are collecting leaves.

Rana collects twice as many as Sarah.

Sarah collects one and a half times as many as Tina.

Between them they collect $198$ leaves.


How many leaves did each hedgehog collect?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the leaves collected by Sarah $= a$

Let the leaves collected by Rana $= 2a$

Let the leaves collected by Tina $= \dfrac {2a}{3} $

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a + \dfrac {2a} {3}                                             &= 198 \\\\
3a + \dfrac {2a} {3}                                                 &= 198 \\\\
\dfrac {3a \times 3}{1 \times 3} + \dfrac {2a} {3}                   &= 198 \\\\
\dfrac {9a + 2a} {3}                                                 &= 198 \\\\
\dfrac {11a} {3}                                                     &= 198 \\\\
a                                                                    &= \dfrac {198 \times 3} {11} \\\\\
a                                                                    &= \dfrac {18 \times 11 \times 3} {11} \\\\
a                                                                    &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel {11} \times 3} {\cancel {11}} \\\\
a                                                                    &= 18 \times 3 \\\\
a                                                                    &= 54
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Leaves collected by Sarah}     &= 54 \\\\
\text {Leaves collected by Rana}      &= 54 \times 2 \\\\
                                      &= 108 \\\\
\text {Leaves collected by Tina}      &= \dfrac {2 \times 54} {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                      &= 18 \times 2 \\\\
                                      &= 36
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$54, 108, 36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the leaves collected by Sarah $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the leaves collected by Rana $= 2a$
<emptyline>
Let the leaves collected by Tina $= \dfrac {2a}{3} $
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a + \dfrac {2a} {3}                                             &= 198 \\\\
3a + \dfrac {2a} {3}                                                 &= 198 \\\\
\dfrac {3a \times 3}{1 \times 3} + \dfrac {2a} {3}                   &= 198 \\\\
\dfrac {9a + 2a} {3}                                                 &= 198 \\\\
\dfrac {11a} {3}                                                     &= 198 \\\\
a                                                                    &= \dfrac {198 \times 3} {11} \\\\\
a                                                                    &= \dfrac {18 \times 11 \times 3} {11} \\\\
a                                                                    &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel {11} \times 3} {\cancel {11}} \\\\
a                                                                    &= 18 \times 3 \\\\
a                                                                    &= 54
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Leaves collected by Sarah}     &= 54 \\\\
\text {Leaves collected by Rana}      &= 54 \times 2 \\\\
                                      &= 108 \\\\
\text {Leaves collected by Tina}      &= \dfrac {2 \times 54} {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                      &= 18 \times 2 \\\\
                                      &= 36
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$54, 108, 36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>77F62AEF-602C-4E6C-8EBE-202B89E04308</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1:1:44</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>

<ul>
<li>
tratio

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

In the same time that Tom can run $100 \ \text{metres}$, Jerry can run only $60 \ \text{metres}$.

Tom and Jerry have a competition.

When Jerry has run $100 \ \text{metres}$, Tom sets off in pursuit.

How many metres will Tom run before he catches Jerry?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the distance covered by Tom $= a$

Distance covered by Jerry $= a - 100$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
100 \ \text{m} : 60 \ \text{m}                  &= a \ \text{m} : (a - 100) \ \text{m} \\\\
\dfrac{100} {60}                                &= \dfrac {a} {a - 100} \\\\
100 \times (a - 100)                            &= a \times 60 \\\\
100a - 10000                                    &= 60a \\\\
100a - 60a                                      &= 10000 \\\\
40a                                             &= 10000 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10000} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {40 \times 250} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times 250} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
a                                               &= 250 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$250 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the distance covered by Tom $= a$
<emptyline>
Distance covered by Jerry $= a - 100$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
100 \ \text{m} : 60 \ \text{m}                  &= a \ \text{m} : (a - 100) \ \text{m} \\\\
\dfrac{100} {60}                                &= \dfrac {a} {a - 100} \\\\
100 \times (a - 100)                            &= a \times 60 \\\\
100a - 10000                                    &= 60a \\\\
100a - 60a                                      &= 10000 \\\\
40a                                             &= 10000 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10000} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {40 \times 250} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times 250} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
a                                               &= 250 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$250 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
